4.12
Bibliographic resources

One of the first a posteriori error analyses for finite volume methods was performed by Angermann
[
20
]
. Later, Achdou
et al.

[
3
]
, Afif
et al.

[
6
]
, Lazarov and Tomov
[
178
]
, Carstensen
et al.

[
73
]
, and Amaziane
et al.

[
18
]
also considered the so-called vertex-centered finite volume discretizations, closely related to lowest-order finite elements. Cell-centered finite volumes of the form (
4.19a
) were then addressed in Agouzal and Oudin
[
8
]
, Achdou
et al.

[
3
]
, Nicaise
[
196
]
, Vohralík
[
249
]
, and Erath and Praetorius
[
116
]
.

5
Steady linear pure diffusion problems. Polytopal meshes and inexpensive implementation and evaluation

We describe here first a model steady linear problem, the Darcy flow problem. This extends the Poisson equation (
4.1
) from Section
4
and forms the basis for complex multiphase compositional porous media flows as that of Section
7
. We also focus on extending the finite volume discretizations on simplicial meshes from Section
4.7
to general polytopal meshes. Finally, we investigate how the guaranteed a posteriori error estimates of the form of Theorem
4.10
can be implemented and evaluated in the most inexpensive way, and this on polytopal meshes. In particular, the flux and potential reconstructions of Sections
4.8
–
4.9
will only be performed here virtually, avoiding their factual construction. Instead, the a posteriori error estimates will only need
one flux value per polytopal face
and
one potential value per vertex of a (virtual) simplicial submesh
. The former are directly available from the numerical scheme (these are the generalizations of the face normal fluxes
U_{{K},{\sigma}}
in (
4.19
)) and the latter will be trivially obtained by some postprocessing and averaging of the elementwise potential values generalizing
p_{K}
in (
4.19
).

5.1
Singlephase steady linear Darcy flow

Consider the problem of finding
p:\Omega{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\nabla{\cdot}({\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla p)

\displaystyle=f\qquad\mbox{ in }\,\Omega,

(5.1a)

\displaystyle\displaystyle p

\displaystyle=0\qquad\mbox{ on }\,\partial\Omega.

(5.1b)

Here
f
is a source term and
{\bm{\underline{K}}}
is a symmetric, positive
definite, and bounded diffusion tensor with values in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
; we suppose for simplicity here that both
f
and
{\bm{\underline{K}}}
are piecewise
constant with respect to the polytopal mesh
\mathcal{T}_{H}
.

5.2
Weak solution and its properties

The Sobolev space
H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
from Definition
3.4
still gives a proper mathematical setting to define a unique solution
p
of problem (
5.1
), which is
p\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
such that

({\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla p,\nabla v)=(f,v)\qquad\forall v\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega).

(5.2)

From the pressure head
p
, we can define the Darcy velocity

\bm{u}:=-{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla p;

(5.3)

recall that we refer to
p
as
potential
and to
\bm{u}
as
flux
. As in Proposition
4.1
, it follows from (
5.2
) that

\bm{u}\in\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega),\quad\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}=f.

(5.4)

Thus, just like in Remark
4.3
, the scalar-valued pressure head
p
is trace continuous, cf. Figure
1
, whereas the vector-valued Darcy velocity
\bm{u}
is normal-trace continuous, cf. Figure
2
. Moreover,
\bm{u}
is locally mass conservative, in equilibrium with the load, satisfying
\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}=f
.

5.3
Spatial discretization error

Applying the abstract reflections of Section
2.4.2
, we will evaluate the spatial discretization error as

\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

(5.5)

for an approximate Darcy velocity
\bm{u}_{h}
.
Here, the energy norm on
\omega\subset\Omega
, for
{\bm{v}}\in\left[L^{2}(\omega)\right]^{d}
, writes as

\|\bm{v}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\omega}:=\|{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1/2}\bm{v}\|_{L^{2}(\omega)}=\left\{\int_{\omega}\left|{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1/2}(\bm{x}){\bm{v}}(\bm{x})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}};

(5.6)

when
\omega=\Omega
, we as usual drop the subscript
\omega
.

5.4
Generic discretizations on a polytopal mesh

Let
\mathcal{T}_{H}
be a polytopal mesh of
\Omega
in the sense of
Section
3.6
. Define the load vector
\mathsf{F}:=\{\mathsf{F}_{K}\}_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{T}_{H}|}

by

\mathsf{F}_{K}:=(f,1)_{K}\qquad\forall{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}.

(5.7)

We consider any numerical discretization of (
5.1
) that can be written under the following abstract lowest-order locally conservative form:

Assumption 5.1

(Locally conservative discretization on a polytopal mesh)
.

Find the algebraic vector
\mathsf{P}:=\{\mathsf{P}_{K}\}_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{T}_{H}|}
such that the flux balance

\boxed{\sum_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\bm{n}_{K}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma}=\mathsf{F}_{K}\quad\forall{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}

(5.8)

is satisfied. Here
\mathsf{U}:=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{H}|}
, and
\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\in{\mathbb{R}}
for each face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
approximates the normal flux
\langle\bm{u}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma},1\rangle_{\sigma}
over the face
{\sigma}
and depends linearly on
\mathsf{P}
.
The unknowns
\mathsf{P}_{K}\in{\mathbb{R}}
for each element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
approximate the potential

p
in the element
{K}
.

Assumption
5.1
is very generic (allows for any lowest-order locally conservative method). Note in particular that for the a posteriori error analysis performed below, we
do not need to know
how the flux unknowns
\mathsf{U}
depend on the pressure head unknowns
\mathsf{P}
. Recall that on admissible simplicial meshes, the easiest example for this relation is the two-point formula (
4.19b
)–(
4.19c
).

Many lowest-order locally conservative methods take a form comprised in Assumption
5.1
but more specific. We consider them
separately under the following assumption, in the spirit of mimetic finite differences
[
64
, Theorem 5.1]
, mixed finite elements
[
254
, Theorems 7.2 and 7.3]
, and the unifying frameworks
[
110
,
111
]
:

Assumption 5.2

(Locally conservative saddle-point discretization on a polytopal mesh)
.

Find the algebraic vectors
\mathsf{U}:=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{H}|}
and
\mathsf{P}:=\{\mathsf{P}_{K}\}_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{T}_{H}|}

such that

\boxed{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{A}&\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{t}}\\
\mathbb{B}&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{U}\\
\mathsf{P}\\
\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{0}\\
-\mathsf{F}\\
\end{pmatrix},}

(5.9)

where

1)

the unknown
\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\in{\mathbb{R}}
for each face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
approximates the normal
flux
\langle\bm{u}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma},1\rangle_{\sigma}
over the face
{\sigma}
and the unknown
\mathsf{P}_{K}\in{\mathbb{R}}
for each element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
approximates the potential
p
in the element
{K}
;

2)

the matrix
\mathbb{B}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{T}_{H}|\times|\mathcal{F}_{H}|}
has a full rank;

3)

for each polytopal cell
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
and face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
,
\mathbb{B}_{{K},{\sigma}}=-\bm{n}_{K}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma}
if
{\sigma}
is a face of
{K}
,
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}
, and
\mathbb{B}_{{K},{\sigma}}=0
otherwise;

4)

the matrix
\mathbb{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{H}|\times|\mathcal{F}_{H}|}
is invertible;

5)

for faces
{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
,
\mathbb{A}_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}=0
if
{\sigma}
and
{\sigma^{\prime}}
are not faces of the same element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
,
\{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}\}\not\subset\mathcal{F}_{{K}}
for some
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, and

\mathbb{A}_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}=\sum_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H},\{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}\}\subset\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}\bm{n}_{K}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma}\,\bm{n}_{K}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K})_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}

(5.10)

otherwise, where
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{{K}}|\times|\mathcal{F}_{{K}}|}
are symmetric and positive definite (the “element matrices” of the given
method for each
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
);

6)

on each polytopal cell
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, there exists a lifting
\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}|_{K}\in\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},{K})
of the face normal fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}
to the interior of
{K}
such that

⟨
~

u

_h—_K
⋅
n
_σ, 1
⟩
_σ =
U
_σ
∀
σ
∈
F
_K,

∇⋅
~

u

_h—_K = f—_K,

∥
~

u

_h
∥
_
K
^-

1

2

,K^2 = (
U
_K^
ext
)^
t

^

A

_K
U
_K^
ext
.

\ea

5.5
Face normal fluxes

In Theorem
5.9
below, we will need a vector of real values with one value
\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}
per face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h}
(approximate face normal fluxes):

Definition 5.3

(Face normal fluxes)
.

Let a discretization scheme of the form of Assumption
5.1
or
5.2
be given, leading to the face normal fluxes
\mathsf{U}:=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{H}|}
. For each polytopal face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
and each simplicial subface
{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h}
as per Section
3.6
, define the face normal flux

\mathsf{U}_{\sigma^{\prime}}:=\frac{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}}{|{\sigma}|}|{\sigma^{\prime}}|\quad\forall{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h},\,{\sigma^{\prime}}\subset{\sigma},\,{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}

(5.12)

and collect them in the element vectors
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}|}

\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}:=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}}

(5.13)

for each polytopal cell
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
.

Remark that if the faces of the simplicial mesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
do not subdivide the
faces of the polytopal mesh
\mathcal{T}_{H}
, then
\mathcal{F}_{H,h}=\mathcal{F}_{H}
and consequently
\mathsf{U}_{\sigma^{\prime}}=\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}
for all
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}
and
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}
.
Figures
7
and
9
give an illustration in two space dimensions.

5.6
Fictitious flux reconstruction by lifting the face normal fluxes

In Definition
4.14
, on simplicial meshes, we have reconstructed a discrete
\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
-conforming flux
\bm{u}_{h}
by lifting the face normal fluxes of a finite volume scheme. We extend here this procedure to general polytopal meshes. In contrast to Theorem
4.10
, however, Theorem
5.9
below will not need
\bm{u}_{h}
to evaluate the posteriori error estimate, so one does
not need to perform
physically/on the computer this reconstruction. Congruently, we may not have a closed formula for the reconstruction, which on top may not be discrete. This is the reason for our naming
fictitious
.

A (fictitious) flux reconstruction under Assumption
5.1

We first treat the generic case of Assumption
5.1
; the case of
Assumption
5.2
is treated below.
Following the concept of lifting operators used in, e.g., Eymard
et al.

[
127
, Section 1.2]
, Kuznetsov and
Repin
[
172
]
, Brezzi
et al.

[
64
, Theorem 5.1]
, Beirão da
Veiga
[
42
, Section 2.1]
, Beirão da Veiga and
Manzini
[
43
, Section 2.4]
,
Kuznetsov
[
173
]
,
Vohralík
[
249
, Section 3.2]
, or Sboui
et al.

[
230
]
, we will extend the fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}
,
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}
, to the interior of each polytopal element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
. The
obtained flux
\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}
will be the approximation of the exact flux
\bm{u}
from (
5.3
) for which we will estimate the energy error
\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}
.
Let us first present a motivation.

Motivation 5.4

(Motivation for the fictitious flux reconstruction)
.

Following
[
249
, equation (3.14)]
, consider in each element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
the infinite-dimensional Darcy problem: for the fluxes
\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}
and the potential
\mathsf{P}_{K}
from Assumption
5.1
, find
\tilde{p}_{K}:{K}{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle-\nabla{\cdot}({\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\tilde{p}_{K})

\displaystyle={f|_{K}},

(5.14a)

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{p}_{K},1)_{K}}{|{K}|}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{P}_{K},

(5.14b)

\displaystyle-{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\tilde{p}_{K}\cdot\bm{n}_{{\sigma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}}{|{\sigma}|}\quad\forall{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}.

(5.14c)

We have actually already seen this problem, in Definition
4.16
of Section
4.9
. There, on a simplicial mesh, the solution was easily practically computable and discrete: the second-order polynomial denoted as
\tilde{p}_{h}|_{K}
(indeed, (
4.24
) gives a solution to (
5.14
)) on a simplicial mesh. Unfortunately, for a general polytopal mesh element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, (
5.14
) amounts to a partial differential equation on
{K}
, with the solution
\tilde{p}_{K}
living in the infinite-dimensional Sobolev space
H^{1}({K})
of Definition
3.3
and the associated flux
\tilde{\bm{u}}_{K}:=-{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\tilde{p}_{K}
living in the infinite-dimensional Sobolev space
\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},{K})
of Definition
3.7
.

Based on Motivation
5.4
, we would like to produce a discrete version of (
5.14
), which will in particular enable us to derive (easily) computable a posteriori error estimates. Following
[
249
, Theorem 5.1]
and
[
254
, Theorems 7.2 and 7.3]
, we consider the mixed finite element method on the simplicial submesh
\mathcal{T}_{K}
of
{K}
to
approximate the solution

\tilde{p}_{K}
of (
5.14
).
Note that this is to be done separately for each
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, so it is an intrinsically parallel procedure.
Recall Definition
5.3
and consider the following sets and spaces of
piecewise polynomials
over the
simplicial submesh

\mathcal{T}_{K}
of
{K}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}:=\{\bm{v}_{h}\in\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{K})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},{K});\,\langle\bm{v}_{h}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma},1\rangle_{\sigma}=\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\quad\forall{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}\},\\[2.84526pt]
\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{0}}^{K}:=\{\bm{v}_{h}\in\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{K})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},{K});\,\langle\bm{v}_{h}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma},1\rangle_{\sigma}=0\quad\forall{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}\},\\[2.84526pt]
Q_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}:=\{q_{h}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{K});\,\frac{(q_{h},1)_{K}}{|{K}|}=\mathsf{P}_{K}\},\\[2.84526pt]
Q_{h,0}^{K}:=\{q_{h}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{K});\,(q_{h},1)_{K}=0\}.\end{array}

(5.15)

Here,
\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}
and
\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{0}}^{K}
are subsets/subspaces of the lowest-order Raviart–Thomas spaces as defined in Section
3.5.3
, whereas
Q_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}
and
Q_{h,0}^{K}
are subsets/subspaces of the piecewise polynomial spaces as defined in Section
3.5.1
. We then let:

Definition 5.5

(Flux reconstruction on polytopal meshes under Assumption
5.1
)
.

Let
f
be constant on each
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
and let a discretization scheme of the form of Assumption
5.1
be given, leading to the normal face fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}
,
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, and consequently to
\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}}
as per Definition
5.3
. For all
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, define
\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}
by the mixed finite element discretization of (
5.14
), described by the constrained quadratic minimization problem

\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}:=\arg\min_{\bm{v}_{h}\in\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K},\,\nabla{\cdot}\bm{v}_{h}=\mathrm{constant}=f|_{K}}\|\bm{v}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}.

(5.16)

Equivalently, writing the Euler–Lagrange conditions of (
5.16
) and imposing the divergence constraint with a Lagrange multiplier,
\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}\in\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}
together with
p_{h}|_{K}\in Q_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}
are given by

\displaystyle({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{u}_{h},\bm{v}_{h})_{K}-(p_{h},\nabla{\cdot}\bm{v}_{h})_{K}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\forall\bm{v}_{h}\in\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{0}}^{K},

(5.17a)

\displaystyle-(\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h},q_{h})_{K}

\displaystyle=-(f,q_{h})_{K}=0

\displaystyle\forall q_{h}\in Q_{h,0}^{K}.

(5.17b)

It is worth noting that the divergence constraint in (
5.16
) can be written with either a generic constant, or more specifically with
f|_{K}
; indeed, the assumption that
f
is constant on each
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, the definition (
5.15
) of
\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}
, and the flux
balance (
5.8
) give the equivalence.
Congruently, (
5.17b
) can be equivalently written as

(\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h},q_{h})_{K}=(f,q_{h})_{K}\qquad\forall q_{h}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{K}),

which is further equivalent to
\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}=f|_{K}
.
Note also that since the normal fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}
are univalued on the faces from
\mathcal{F}_{H,h}
, the resulting flux reconstruction
\bm{u}_{h}
has the normal trace continuous over all interior mesh faces from
\mathcal{F}_{H,h}
and thus belongs to the
Raviart–Thomas space
\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
associated with the
simplicial mesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
of the entire domain
\Omega
. In consequence, we have:

Lemma 5.6

(Flux reconstruction on polytopal meshes under Assumption
5.1
)
.

Let
f
be constant on each
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
. Then the flux reconstruction of Definition
5.5
satisfies

\bm{u}_{h}\in\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)\quad\text{ with }\quad\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h}=f.

Figure 17:
Face barycentres
\bm{x}_{{K},{\sigma}}
and vertices
{\bm{a}}_{{K},{\sigma}}
opposite to a face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}
. Simplex
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{h}
in space dimensions
d=2
(left) and
d=3
(right)

Construction 5.7

(Matrix form of problem (
5.17
))
.

Let
\bm{v}_{\sigma}
,

{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int}
, be the basis functions of the space
\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{0}}^{K}
, and
\bm{v}_{\sigma}
,
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}
, be the remaining basis
functions of the space
\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{K})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},{K})
, see (
3.13
) and Figures
6
and
17
. Similarly, let
q_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq|Q_{h,0}^{K}|
, be the basis functions of the space
Q_{h,0}^{K}
(piecewise constants with mean value
0
).
Consider these basis functions of
\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{0}}^{K}
and
Q_{h,0}^{K}
as test functions
\bm{v}_{h}
and
q_{h}
in (
5.17
) and develop

\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}=\underbrace{\sum_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}}(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})_{\sigma}\bm{v}_{\sigma}}_{\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}}}+\underbrace{\sum_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int}}(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{int}})_{\sigma}\bm{v}_{\sigma}}_{\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}}}

and

p_{h}|_{K}=\mathsf{P}_{K}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{|Q_{h,0}^{K}|}(\mathsf{P}^{0}_{K})_{i}q_{i}}_{p_{h,{K}}^{0}},

where
\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}
and
\mathsf{P}_{K}
are the given data of the problem (
5.14
) and the data
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}:=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}}
appearing in problem (
5.16
) are given by Definition
5.3
.
Then (
5.17
) in matrix form corresponds to: find

\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{int}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{0}}^{K}|}
and
\mathsf{P}^{0}_{K}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|Q_{h,0}^{K}|}
such that

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{int}}&(\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{int}})^{\mathrm{t}}\\
\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{int}}&0\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{int}}\\
\mathsf{P}^{0}_{K}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{l}-\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{ext}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}\\
-\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{ext}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}\\
\end{array}\right),

(5.18)

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{int}}

\displaystyle\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int}|\times|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int}|},\qquad

\displaystyle(\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{int}})_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle:=({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{v}_{\sigma^{\prime}},\bm{v}_{\sigma})_{K}\qquad

\displaystyle{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int},

(5.19a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{ext}}

\displaystyle\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int}|\times|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}|}

\displaystyle(\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{ext}})_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle:=({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{v}_{\sigma^{\prime}},\bm{v}_{\sigma})_{K}

\displaystyle{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int},\,{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext},

(5.19b)

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{int}}

\displaystyle\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|Q_{h,0}^{K}|\times|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int}|}

\displaystyle(\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{int}})_{i,{\sigma^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle:=-(\nabla{\cdot}\bm{v}_{\sigma^{\prime}},q_{i})_{K}

\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq|Q_{h,0}^{K}|,\,{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm int},

(5.19c)

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{ext}}

\displaystyle\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|Q_{h,0}^{K}|\times|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}|}

\displaystyle(\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{ext}})_{i,{\sigma^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle:=-(\nabla{\cdot}\bm{v}_{\sigma^{\prime}},q_{i})_{K}

\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq|Q_{h,0}^{K}|,\,{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}.

(5.19d)

It will also be useful to define

(\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{ext}})_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}:=({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{v}_{\sigma^{\prime}},\bm{v}_{\sigma})_{K}\qquad{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}.

Definition
5.5
is practical and we can solve problem (
5.17
) or (
5.18
) to have
\bm{u}_{h}
available. We, however, want to avoid this (unless we want to compute the energy error
\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}
in model cases with known exact solution
\bm{u}
). In Theorem
5.9
below, we will principally only need the energy
\|\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}
. In this respect, we observe that
Definition
5.5
lifts the information from the boundary of each polytopal
element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
given by the fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}
to
the interior of the element
{K}
.
It is thus clear that
\|\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}
must only depend on

\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}
and can be obtained directly therefrom. It turns out that this link can be
expressed by a single element matrix constructed solely from the geometry of the simplicial submesh
\mathcal{T}_{K}
of
{K}
, using Construction
5.7
.
As in
[
254
, proof of Theorem 7.3]
, we indeed
have:

Lemma 5.8

(Energy norm and MFE element matrix)
.

Let a discretization scheme of the form of Assumption
5.1
be given, leading to the normal face fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}
,
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, and consequently to
\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}}
as per Definition
5.3
. For each

{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, let
\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}\in\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}
be given by
Definition
5.5
. Relying on Construction
5.7
, define the element matrix of the mixed finite element method

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}:=\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{ext}}-\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{ext}}\\
\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{ext}}\\
\end{array}\right)^{\mathrm{t}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{int}}&(\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{int}})^{\mathrm{t}}\\
\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{int}}&0\\
\end{array}\right)^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{ext}}\\
\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{ext}}\\
\end{array}\right){,}

(5.20)

where the building matrices are given by (
5.19
).
Then

\|\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}=(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}.

(5.21)

Proof.

Let
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
be a fixed polytopal mesh element. Following Construction
5.7
, decompose

\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}=\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}}+\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}}

and
p_{h}|_{K}=\mathsf{P}_{K}+p_{h,{K}}^{0}
. Note that choosing
\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}}\in\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{0}}^{K}
as the test function
in (
5.17a
) and
p_{h,{K}}^{0}\in Q_{h,0}^{K}
as the test
function in (
5.17b
), one has

({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{u}_{h},\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}})_{K}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq_fl_equil_1}}}{{=}}(p_{h},\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}})_{K}=}(p_{h,{K}}^{0},\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}})_{K}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq_fl_equil_2}}}{{=}}}-(p_{h,{K}}^{0},\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}})_{K},

where we have also used
(\mathsf{P}_{K},\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}})_{K}=0
in the middle equality.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}

\displaystyle={({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{u}_{h},\bm{u}_{h})_{K}}

\displaystyle=({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{u}_{h},\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}})_{K}+({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}},\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}})_{K}+({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}},\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}})_{K}

\displaystyle=-(p_{h,{K}}^{0},\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}})_{K}+({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{int}},\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}})_{K}+({\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1}\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}},\bm{u}_{h,{K}}^{\mathrm{ext}})_{K}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{ext}}\\
\mathbb{B}_{K}^{0,\mathrm{ext}}\\
\end{array}\right)^{\mathrm{t}}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{int}}\\
\mathsf{P}^{0}_{K}\\
\end{array}\right)+(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{ext}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}.

Combining this with the MFE matrix form (
5.18
) and the
MFE element matrix definition (
5.20
) yields the claim (
5.21
). ∎

A fictitious flux reconstruction under Assumption
5.2

There exist other ways how to lift the face normal fluxes

\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}
into the interior of each polytope
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
than via Definition
5.5
. For instance, one could use a finite volume discretization of (
5.14
) (like (
4.19a
)) in place of the mixed finite element one (
5.17
). Under Assumption
5.2
, which defines
\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}
, a much more direct and simple procedure suggests itself: to use directly the lifting operator from (
5.11
). This is in particular the theoretical vehicle in mimetic finite differences according to, e.g.,
[
64
, Theorem 5.1]
. In contrast to (
5.16
), where we can solve for
\bm{u}_{h}
if needed, the reconstruction
\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}
we now introduce may be truly fictitious in the sense that it may not be practically possible to construct it. Thus, we will not be able to compute the actual error
\|\bm{u}-\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}
in model cases with known exact flux
\bm{u}
, but otherwise this not be an issue (we will still be able to compute the a posteriori error estimate of Theorem
5.9
below). In particular, thanks to (
5.11
), we have the following trivial counterpart of Lemma
5.8
:

Lemma 5.9

(Energy norm and given discretization scheme element matrix)
.

Let a discretization scheme of the form of
Assumption
5.2
be given, leading to the element matrices

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K}
and normal face fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}
,
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
. Then the lifting
\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}
of (
5.11
) satisfies

\|\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}=(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}.

(5.22)

Following
[
254
, Theorem 7.3]
, the mixed finite element element matrix
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
defined by (
5.20
)
belongs to the same family as the element matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K}

of mimetic finite differences, mixed finite volumes, and hybrid finite
volumes of Assumption
5.2
when the simplicial
faces of
\mathcal{T}_{h}
do not subdivide the faces of the polytopal mesh
\mathcal{T}_{H}
.
This leads to the following strong interconnection of Lemmas
5.8
and
5.9
(this is an illuminating observation not needed in practice/thory):

Lemma 5.10

(Approximate energy norm)
.

Let the assumptions of Lemmas
5.8
and
5.9
and of
[
64
, Theorem 5.1]
be satisfied. Let the simplicial
faces of
\mathcal{T}_{h}
do not subdivide the faces of the polytopal mesh
\mathcal{T}_{H}
.
Then there exist two constants
c,C>0
independent of the matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
, and the mesh size but possibly depending on the shape-regularity of the simplicial submesh
\mathcal{T}_{K}
such that

c\underbrace{{\|\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}}}_{(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}}\leq\underbrace{\|\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}}_{{(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}}}\leq C\underbrace{{\|\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}}}_{(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}}

(5.23)

that we denote by

\|\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}\approx(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}.

(5.24)

Proof.

Any scheme of the form of Assumption
5.2
with
the lifting operator according to
[
64
, Theorem 5.1]
satisfies the equality (5.7) from
[
64
]
, i.e., (
5.11
). As mixed finite elements
on polytopal meshes belong to the mimetic finite difference family by

[
254
, Theorem 7.3]
, this implies (
5.23
). ∎

5.7
Potential point values

In Theorem
5.9
below, we will need a vector of real values with one value
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}
per vertex
{\bm{a}}\in\mathcal{V}_{h}
of the simplicial submesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
of
\mathcal{T}_{H}
and one real value
\mathsf{Z}_{{\sigma}}
per face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h}
of the simplicial submesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
of
\mathcal{T}_{H}
(approximate point values of the pressure heads). We identify them here for the two Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
.

Potential point values under Assumption
5.1

Here, under the general Assumption
5.1
, we define the vectors
\{\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}\}_{{\bm{a}}\in\mathcal{V}_{h}}
and
\{\mathsf{Z}_{{\sigma}}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h}}
by average values of
\mathsf{P}_{K}
in the neighboring polytopal elements:

Definition 5.11

(Potential point values on polytopal meshes under Assumption
5.1
)
.

Let a discretization scheme of the form of Assumption
5.1
be given, leading to the elementwise pressure heads
\mathsf{P}:=\{\mathsf{P}_{K}\}_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}
. For each vertex
{\bm{a}}
of the simplicial mesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}

lying on
\partial{K}
for some
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
but not on
\partial\Omega
, let
\mathcal{T}_{\bm{a}}
denote the set of
polytopal elements
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
sharing
{\bm{a}}
. We set

\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{T}_{\bm{a}}|}\sum_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{\bm{a}}}\mathsf{P}_{K}{,}

(5.25a)

where, recall,
|\mathcal{T}_{\bm{a}}|
is the cardinality of the set
\mathcal{T}_{\bm{a}}
, i.e., the number of
polytopal elements
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
sharing
{\bm{a}}
.
We also set

Z
_
a
:=
P
_K
for any vertex
{\bm{a}}
of
\mathcal{T}_{h}
lying inside some
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
,

Z
_
a
:=0
for any vertex
{\bm{a}}
lying simultaneously on
\partial{K}
for some
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
and on
\partial\Omega
,

\ea

where the latter condition is set in accordance with the boundary condition (
5.1b
).
We will also need the face values
\{\mathsf{Z}_{{\sigma}}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h}}

\mathsf{Z}_{{\sigma}}:=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{{\bm{a}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\sigma}}\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}

(5.26)

for any simplicial face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h}
, where, recall,
\mathcal{V}_{\sigma}
collects the vertices of the given face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h}
and
d
is the space dimension.
Then, for each polytopal cell
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, we let the element vector
\mathsf{Z}_{K}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}|}
collect the values
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}
for the vertices
{\bm{a}}
of the simplicial mesh
\mathcal{T}_{K}
lying
on the boundary
\partial{K}
and the value(s) inside
{K}

\mathsf{Z}_{K}:=\{\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}\}_{{\bm{a}}\in\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}}

(5.27)

and the element vector
\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}|}
collect the values associated
with faces
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}

\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}:=\{\mathsf{Z}_{{\sigma}}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}}.

(5.28)

Figure
18
gives an illustration in two space dimensions.

\bullet
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{8}}
\bullet
\times
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{1}}
\mathsf{Z}_{\sigma_{1}}
\bullet
\times
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{2}}
\mathsf{Z}_{\sigma_{2}}
\bullet
\times
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{3}}
\mathsf{Z}_{\sigma_{3}}
\bullet
\times
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{4}}
\mathsf{Z}_{\sigma_{4}}
\bullet
\times
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{5}}
\mathsf{Z}_{\sigma_{5}}
\bullet
\times
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{6}}
\mathsf{Z}_{\sigma_{6}}
\bullet
\times
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{7}}
\mathsf{Z}_{\sigma_{7}}
\mathcal{V}_{K}=\{{\bf a}_{i}\}^{7}_{i=1}
\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}=\{{\bf a}_{i}\}^{8}_{i=1}
\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}=\{\sigma_{i}\}^{7}_{i=1}
\mathsf{Z}_{K}=\{\mathsf{Z}_{{\bf a}_{i}}\}^{8}_{i=1}
\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{K}=\{\mathsf{Z}_{\sigma_{i}}\}^{7}_{i=1}

Figure 18:
Example of element vectors
\mathsf{Z}_{K}
and
\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{K}
of nodal and facial potential point values

Potential point values under Assumption
5.2

A more straightforward and precise potential reconstruction can be designed under Assumption
5.2
. The main idea is
that any scheme of the form (
5.9
) can be hybridized,
following, e.g., Roberts and Thomas
[
224
]
, Brezzi and
Fortin
[
63
]
, or Droniou
et al.

[
110
]
,
giving rise to one Lagrange multiplier
\Lambda_{\sigma}
per face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
:

Definition 5.12

(Lagrange multipliers under Assumption
5.2
)
.

Let a discretization scheme of the form of Assumption
5.2
be given and fix a face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
.
Consider the line associated with
{\sigma}
in the first block equation of (
5.9
). For an interior face
{\sigma}
, let it be shared by two polytopes
{K},{L}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
such that
\bm{n}_{\sigma}
points from
{K}
to
{L}
. From the structure of the matrices
\mathbb{A}

and
\mathbb{B}
supposed in Assumption
5.2
, it follows that

\sum_{{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}\bm{n}_{K}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K})_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}\mathsf{U}_{\sigma^{\prime}}-\mathsf{P}_{K}=\sum_{{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\bm{n}_{{L},{\sigma^{\prime}}}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{L}})_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}\mathsf{U}_{\sigma^{\prime}}-\mathsf{P}_{L},

so that these expressions are univalued from both elements
{K}
and
{L}
. Such an expression is also clearly univalued on boundary faces. It allows to define the Lagrange multipliers

\Lambda_{\sigma}:=\mathsf{P}_{K}-\sum_{{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}}\bm{n}_{K}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K})_{{\sigma},{\sigma^{\prime}}}\mathsf{U}_{\sigma^{\prime}}

(5.29)

for each element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
and each face
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}
.

Definition 5.13

(Potential point values on polytopal meshes under Assumption
5.2
)
.

Let a discretization scheme of the form of Assumption
5.2
be given, leading to the normal face fluxes
\mathsf{U}=\{\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}}
and the elementwise pressure heads
\mathsf{P}:=\{\mathsf{P}_{K}\}_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}
. Let the Lagrange multipliers
\Lambda_{\sigma}
be given by Definition
5.12
.
For each vertex
{\bm{a}}
of the simplicial mesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}

lying on
\partial{K}
for some
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
but not on
\partial\Omega
, let
\mathcal{F}_{\bm{a}}
denote the set of
polytopal faces
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H}
sharing
{\bm{a}}
and
|\mathcal{F}_{\bm{a}}|
its cardinality. We set

\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{\bm{a}}|}\sum_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{\bm{a}}}\Lambda_{\sigma}.

(5.30a)

We also set

Z
_
a
:=
P
_K
for any vertex
{\bm{a}}
of
\mathcal{T}_{h}
lying
inside some
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
,

Z
_
a
:=0
for any vertex
{\bm{a}}
lying simultaneously on
\partial{K}
for some
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
and on
\partial\Omega
,

\ea

where the latter condition is set in accordance with the boundary condition (
5.1b
).
We then define the face values
\{\mathsf{Z}_{{\sigma}}\}_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{H,h}}
and the element vectors
\mathsf{Z}_{K}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}|}
and
\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}|}
by respectively (
5.26
), (
5.27
), and (
5.28
) as in Definition
5.11
.

5.8
Fictitious potential reconstruction by respecting the point values

In Definition
4.17
, we have reconstructed a discrete
H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
-conforming potential
\zeta_{h}
from a finite volume approximation on a simplicial mesh. We extend here this procedure to general polytopal meshes. As in Section
5.6
, in contrast to Theorem
4.10
, however, Theorem
5.9
below will not need
\zeta_{h}
to evaluate the posteriori error estimate, so one again does
not need to perform
physically/on the computer this reconstruction. This is the reason for our naming
fictitious
(though we will give a closed formula for
\zeta_{h}
and
\zeta_{h}
will be discrete and possible to construct if desired).
Recall the notation from Section
3.6
.

Given the point values
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}
, either prescribed by (
5.25
) from Definition
5.11
or by (
5.30
) of Definition
5.13
, the potential reconstruction
\zeta_{h}{\in{\mathcal{P}}_{1}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)}
(piecewise
affine with respect to the simplicial submesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
and
H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
-conforming) is trivial:

Definition 5.14

(Potential reconstruction on polytopal meshes)
.

Let the potential point values
\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}
be given by either Definition
5.11
or Definition
5.13
.
Then the potential reconstruction
\zeta_{h}{\in{\mathcal{P}}_{1}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)}
is prescribed by

\zeta_{h}({\bm{a}}):=\mathsf{Z}_{{\bm{a}}}\qquad\forall{\bm{a}}\in\mathcal{V}_{h}.

Note that by (
5.26
), since
\zeta_{h}
is affine on each face,

(\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}})_{\sigma}=\mathsf{Z}_{{\sigma}}=\zeta_{h}(\bm{x}_{\sigma}),

(5.31)

i.e., the face values
\mathsf{Z}_{{\sigma}}
are the punctual values of the reconstruction
\zeta_{h}
in the face barycenters
\bm{x}_{\sigma}
,
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}
, cf. Figure
17
.

Consider the usual hat basis functions
\psi_{\bm{a}}
of the space
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap H^{1}(\Omega)
on the simplicial mesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
:
\psi_{\bm{a}}
is piecewise affine on
\mathcal{T}_{h}
and
H^{1}(\Omega)
-conforming,

\psi_{\bm{a}}({\bm{a}^{\prime}})=1
if
{\bm{a}}={\bm{a}^{\prime}}
and
0
otherwise, where
{\bm{a}},{\bm{a}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{V}_{h}
are the vertices of
\mathcal{T}_{h}
. For any polytopal element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, we will need below the element
stiffness matrix

\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}|\times|\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}|}
defined by

(\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}})_{{\bm{a}},{\bm{a}^{\prime}}}:=({\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\psi_{\bm{a}^{\prime}},\nabla\psi_{\bm{a}})_{K}\qquad{\bm{a}},{\bm{a}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}

(5.32)

and the element
mass matrix

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}|\times|\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}|}
defined by

(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}})_{{\bm{a}},{\bm{a}^{\prime}}}:=(\psi_{\bm{a}^{\prime}},\psi_{\bm{a}})_{K}\qquad{\bm{a}},{\bm{a}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{V}_{{K},h}.

(5.33)

An immediate consequence is:

Lemma 5.15

(Energy norm and integration using the stiffness and mass matrices)
.

Let the potential reconstruction be given by Definition
5.14
and the stiffness and mass matrices respectively by (
5.32
) and (
5.33
). Then there holds

\|{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\zeta_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}=\mathsf{Z}_{K}^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}{\mathsf{Z}_{K}}

(5.34)

and

(1,\zeta_{h})_{K}=\mathsf{1}^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}{\mathsf{Z}_{K}}.

(5.35)

Remark 5.16

(Element matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
and

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
)
.

To
obtain the finite element stiffness and mass matrices

\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
of (
5.32
) and

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
of (
5.33
), one
could use a finite element assembly code linked to a simplicial mesh on the
polytopal cell
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
. Note, however, that there exist simple
analytical formulas for the basis functions
\psi_{\bm{a}}
of piecewise affine Lagrange
finite elements and for their gradients
\nabla\psi_{\bm{a}}
that merely
necessitate the position of the vertices of the polygonal element
{K}
and
of the central point. In this sense, we say that neither
\mathcal{T}_{h}
nor
\mathcal{T}_{K}

need not be constructed in practice in order to obtain

\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
and

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
.

We finish this section by a remark on an alternative potential reconstruction in the spirit of Definitions
4.16
–
4.17
. This is still more precise, though slightly more computationally demanding; we will use it for comparison in numerical experiments below.

Remark 5.17

(Piecewise quadratic potential reconstruction)
.

Let

(\bm{u}_{h}|_{K},p_{h}|_{K})
be given by Definition
5.5
on all
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
.
We let
\tilde{p}_{h}
be a piecewise
quadratic polynomial on the simplicial mesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
given by

-{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\tilde{p}_{h}|_{\kappa}=\bm{u}_{h}|_{\kappa},\qquad\frac{(\tilde{p}_{h},1)_{\kappa}}{|\kappa|}=p_{h}|_{\kappa}\qquad\forall\kappa\in\mathcal{T}_{h}.

Then a piecewise quadratic potential reconstruction
\zeta_{h}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
can
be obtained by averaging the values of
\tilde{p}_{h}
in all Lagrange nodes of
freedom as per Definition
4.17
.

5.9
A guaranteed a posteriori error estimate with inexpensive implementation and evaluation

We now present our a posteriori error estimate for the model problem (
5.1
). Our main result is given for the error between the exact Darcy velocity
\bm{u}\in\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
of (
5.2
)–(
5.3
) and the reconstruction
\bm{u}_{h}\in\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
of Definition
5.5
evaluated as per (
5.5
), under the general Assumption
5.1
:

Theorem 5.18
(A guaranteed a posteriori error estimate with inexpensive implementation and evaluation)
.

Let
f
be constant on each
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
and let the exact flux
\bm{u}
be given by (
5.2
)–(
5.3
).
For any polytopal discretization satisfying Assumption
5.1
, let, for each polytopal element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, the element vectors of face normal fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}
be given by Definition
5.3
.
Let the element vectors of potential point values
\mathsf{Z}_{K}
and
\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}
be given by Definition
5.11
.
Let finally the matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
,

\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
, and

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
be respectively defined
by (
5.20
), (
5.32
),
and (
5.33
).
Then there holds

\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\Biggl\{\sum_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}\eta_{K}^{2}\Biggr\}^{1/2},

(5.36)

where

\eta_{K}^{2}:=(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}+\mathsf{Z}_{K}^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}\mathsf{Z}_{K}+2(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}-2{\mathsf{F}_{K}|{K}|^{-1}}\mathsf{1}^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}\mathsf{Z}_{K}.

(5.37)

Here the flux reconstruction
\bm{u}_{h}\in\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
with
\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h}=f
is obtained following Definition
5.5
.

Proof.

Recall from Lemma
5.6
that
\bm{u}_{h}\in\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
with
\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h}=f
. Thus, considering the diffusion tensor
{\bm{\underline{K}}}
in place of the identity matrix and proceeding as for the Prager–Synge equality of Corollary
4.6
, we have

\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}=\min_{v\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)}\|\bm{u}_{h}+{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla v\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

(5.38)

Consequently, for an arbitrary
\zeta_{h}\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
,

\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\|\bm{u}_{h}+{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\zeta_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

We now choose for
\zeta_{h}
the fictitious potential reconstruction
\zeta_{h}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{1}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
of Definition
5.14
, continuous and piecewise affine with respect to
the simplicial submesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
and given by the nodal values of the
vector
\mathsf{Z}
from Definition
5.11
. We focus on the evaluation of
\|\bm{u}_{h}+{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\zeta_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}
. Developing, we obtain, for each polytopal mesh element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
,

\|\bm{u}_{h}+{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\zeta_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}=\|\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}+2(\bm{u}_{h},\nabla\zeta_{h})_{K}+\|{\bm{\underline{K}}}\nabla\zeta_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},{K}}^{2}.

We now use Lemma
5.8
for the first term
and (
5.34
) from Lemma
5.15
for the last one. For the middle term, recall first
that the normal components of vector fields in
\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
are constant on
each face. Thus the Green theorem together with (
5.26
) and its consequence (
5.31
),
\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h}=f
and (
5.7
), and (
5.35
) from Lemma
5.15
give

(\bm{u}_{h},\nabla\zeta_{h})_{K}=\langle\bm{u}_{h}{\cdot}\bm{n},\zeta_{h}\rangle_{\partial{K}}-(\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h},\zeta_{h})_{K}=(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}-{\mathsf{F}_{K}|{K}|^{-1}}\mathsf{1}^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}\mathsf{Z}_{K}.

(5.39)

Thus the proof is finished. ∎

Note that the a posteriori error estimators
\eta_{K}
of Theorem
5.9
take a simple form of
matrix-vector multiplication
on each polytopal mesh element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, only need the element vectors
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}
,
\mathsf{Z}_{K}
, and
\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}
together with the element matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
,

\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
, and

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
, but
not the reconstructions

\bm{u}_{h}
and
\zeta_{h}
(which become fictitious), and yet (
5.36
) delivers a
guaranteed upper bound
on the Darcy velocity error.

For schemes of the form of Assumption
5.2
, Definition
5.13
can alternatively be used in place of Definition
5.11
in Theorem
5.9
. In this case, however, it is probably still more attractive to proceed as:

Corollary 5.19

(A simple guaranteed estimate with the given element matrices

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
)
.

Let
f
be constant on each
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
and let the exact flux
\bm{u}
be given by (
5.2
)–(
5.3
).
For any polytopal discretization satisfying Assumption
5.2
, let, for each polytopal element
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
, the element vectors of face normal fluxes
\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}
be given by Definition
5.3
.
Let the element vectors of potential point values
\mathsf{Z}_{K}
and
\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}
be given by Definition
5.13
.
Let the matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
be the element matrices from Assumption
5.2
. Let finally
\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
and

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
be respectively defined
by (
5.32
) and (
5.33
).
Then there holds

\|\bm{u}-\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\Biggl\{\sum_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}\eta_{K}^{2}\Biggr\}^{1/2},

(5.40)

where

\eta_{K}^{2}:=(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}}+\mathsf{Z}_{K}^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}\mathsf{Z}_{K}+2(\mathsf{U}_{K}^{\mathrm{ext}})^{\mathrm{t}}\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}-2{\mathsf{F}_{K}|{K}|^{-1}}\mathsf{1}^{\mathrm{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}\mathsf{Z}_{K},

(5.41)

i.e., the same form as (
5.37
) but with
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
in place of
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
. Here the fictitious flux reconstruction
\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}\in\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
with
\nabla{\cdot}\bm{u}_{h}=f
is obtained following (
5.11
).

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
5.9
, using
the facts that
\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}\in\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
,
\nabla{\cdot}\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}=f
, and

\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}{\cdot}\bm{n}_{\sigma}=\mathsf{U}_{\sigma}/|{\sigma}|
for all
{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{{K}}

following (
5.11
), and replacing
Lemma
5.8
by Lemma
5.9
. ∎

Finally, relying on Lemma
5.10
, we also have:

Corollary 5.20

(A simple estimate with the given element matrices

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
)
.

Let the
assumptions of Corollary
5.19
hold, let
\mathcal{F}_{{K},h}^{\rm ext}=\mathcal{F}_{{K}}
,
i.e., the simplicial faces of
\mathcal{T}_{h}
do not subdivide the faces of the
polytopal mesh
\mathcal{T}_{H}
, and let the flux
\bm{u}_{h}\in\bm{\mathcal{R\hskip-1.00006ptT}}\hskip-2.5pt_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap\bm{H}(\mathrm{div},\Omega)
be
constructed by Definition
5.5
. Then

\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{h}\|_{{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\Biggl\{\sum_{{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}}\eta_{K}^{2}\Biggr\}^{1/2},

(5.42)

where
\eta_{K}
is given by (
5.41
) and the “approximately less
than or equal to” denoted by
\lesssim
stems from the
approximation (
5.24
).

A few comments are in order:

Remark 5.21

(Comparison of Theorem
5.9
with
Corollaries
5.19
and
5.20
)
.

Theorem
5.9
is applicable to any scheme of the form of
Assumption
5.1
and relies on the the mixed finite element
matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
that one needs to construct
by (
5.20
). In contrast, Corollaries
5.19

and
5.20
are only applicable to schemes of the form
of Assumption
5.2
, but use directly the matrices

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
. The estimates (
5.36
)
and (
5.42
) hold for the reconstruction
\bm{u}_{h}
that one
can compute in practice if needed by Definition
5.5
,
whereas (
5.40
) only holds for the generally unavailable
lifting
\tilde{\bm{u}}_{h}
, as
in
[
42
,
43
]
. Finally,
both (
5.36
) and (
5.40
) are guaranteed, with the
\leq
inequality,
whereas (
5.42
) is not. In the numerical experiments in
Section
5.10
below, though, it is hard to distinguish the two
estimators (
5.37
) and (
5.41
).

Remark 5.22

(Simplicial submeshes and solution of local problems)
.

We can avoid
the physical construction of the simplicial submeshes
\mathcal{T}_{K}
under the
conditions that 1) the given scheme takes the form of
Assumption
5.2
; 2) the element matrices

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
are explicitly given; 3) the stiffness matrix

\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
of (
5.32
) and the
mass matrix
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}

of (
5.33
) of the conforming finite element method on

\mathcal{T}_{K}
for each polytopal cell
{K}\in\mathcal{T}_{H}
are constructed from the
geometry of
\mathcal{T}_{K}
only, in the sense of Remark
5.16
; 4) we use
the estimator
\eta_{K}
(
5.41
) of
Corollary
5.19
or
5.20
. Note,
however, that one may need a submesh of each polytopal cell
{K}
already to
produce the scheme element matrix
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
. Similarly, a
typical implementation of
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
(
5.20
) will also need the submesh
\mathcal{T}_{K}
, though it can also be constructed from the geometry of
\mathcal{T}_{K}
only. Remark finally that behind (
5.20
),
there is the local Neumann problem of Definition
5.5
.

Remark 5.23

(Higher-order locally conservative methods)
.

Higher-order locally conservative methods can be treated similarly to the above exposition but are not subject of this work.

5.10
Numerical experiments

The purpose of this section is to numerically illustrate the performance of
the estimators of Theorem
5.9
, as well as the simplified
estimate (
5.42
) of
Corollary
5.20
. We also compare this
methodology to a reference estimate on the simplicial submesh in particular
using the potential reconstruction of Remark
5.17
. The test
is taken from
[
188
]
, where a collection of
two-dimensional elliptic problems for testing adaptive grid refinement
algorithms is proposed. We approximate
-\Delta p={f}
on the space
domain
\Omega=(0,1)^{2}
where the analytical solution is

p(x,y)=2^{4\alpha}x^{\alpha}(1-x)^{\alpha}y^{\alpha}(1-y)^{\alpha},\quad\alpha=200,

(5.43)

see Figure
19
.
The source term
f
prescribed correspondingly and we neglect here that it is not piecewise constant, i.e., the data oscillation term as the second term in (
4.26b
). We use homogeneous Dirichlet
boundary conditions.

Figure 19:
The exact solution (
5.43
)

The mesh
\mathcal{T}_{H}
consists of general polygonal elements; together with the fictitious
triangular submesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
, it is shown in Figure
8
. We consider the
hybrid finite volume (HFV) discretization (Droniou
et al.

[
128
]

or
[
110
, Section 2.2]
), taking the form (
5.9
) of Assumption
5.2
with the matrix
\mathbb{A}
formed by local element
matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K}
. We compare three versions of a posteriori
error estimates: a) Theorem
5.9
, with the
estimators
\eta_{K}
evaluated via the matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
,

\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
, and
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathrm{FE},{K}}
(polygonal MFE estimate); b) Corollary
5.20
, using the element matrix
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{K}}
of the HFV scheme that is already available (polygonal HFV estimate); c) estimate (
5.36
) with the estimators
\eta_{K}
replaced by the expression

\|{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{-1/2}\bm{u}_{h}+{\bm{\underline{K}}}^{1/2}\nabla\zeta_{h}\|_{K}
, where
\bm{u}_{h}|_{K}{\in\bm{V}_{h,\mathrm{N}}^{K}}
is given in Definition
5.5
and
\zeta_{h}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\cap H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
is described in Remark
5.17
(triangular estimate, sharpest-possible, for comparison); In the first two cases, we use Definition
5.11
to obtain the vectors of the potential point values
\mathsf{Z}_{K}
and
\mathsf{Z}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{{K}}
by (
5.25
) and (
5.26
).

Note that if the mixed finite element method on polygonal meshes
of
[
254
, Theorem 7.2]
was used instead of the
HFV discretization, the third procedure would be,
following
[
254
, Remark 7.3]
, fully equivalent
to solving the problem (
5.1
) directly on the simplicial mesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}

by the lowest-order Raviart–Thomas mixed finite element method
and applying the a posteriori error estimates of Theorem
4.10
.
This is why the triangular estimate serves here as a reference a posteriori error estimate. The first procedure only uses the
piecewise affine potential reconstruction
\zeta_{h}
of Definitions
5.11
and
5.14
,
which allows for the simple matrix form (
5.37
) of the estimators. The
second procedure is definitely the easiest choice in practice, where only the
already available element matrices
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K}
are used and
there is no need to construct the mixed finite element matrices

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
via (
5.20
).

(a)
Energy error

(b)
Triangular estimate

(c)
Polygonal MFE estimate

(d)
Polygonal HFV estimate

Figure 20:
Actual and estimated error distributions, entire domain (
top
) and center zoom (
bottom
)

In Figure
20
we compare the actual and predicted error
distributions. The energy error and the sharp triangular estimate distributions match perfectly. The polygonal MFE estimate and the
polygonal HFV estimates give similar results and match also well with the
energy error, though the error distribution around the peak is less well captured. We depict in Figure
21
the error and estimates
as a function of the total number of unknowns and the corresponding
effectivity indices for a uniform mesh refinement. This is in this test
performed as follows: we refine uniformly the triangular submesh
\mathcal{T}_{h}
,
giving actually rise to a Delaunay triangulation on each step, and then we
merge triangles into polygons. All the three estimators behave in a similar
way, with a slight advantage for the triangular estimate. The graphs
confirm in particular that replacing the mixed finite element matrix

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{MFE},{K}}
in (
5.37
) by

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{K}
in (
5.41
) has a very small
influence.
